JJ Gabriel says goodbye to Man Utd amid Real Madrid agreement

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Real Madrid reached an agreement with Manchester United earlier this month to sign JJ Gabriel; The teenager requested to leave United in September and has not trained with the club since; the 16-year-old and his camp are confident there is a clear pathway to the first team at Real Madrid

JJ Gabriel has announced he is leaving Manchester United as he looks forward to his "next chapter" at Real Madrid.

Sky Sports News revealed earlier this month that Real have reached an agreement to sign the 16-year-old prodigy from United.

Gabriel is registered with Manchester United's academy until the end of the season but Real Madrid are intending to sign him in January.

There is a FIFA loophole that allows European clubs to tie down young players to professional contracts if they have a European passport which Gabriel qualifies for through both parents.

In a farewell post on Instagram he said: "Dear Manchester United, I arrived in 2020. as a 10-year-old boy with big dreams.

"Here I grew as a player and as a person. From scoring at Old Trafford to receiving the Jimmy Murphy Award.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"You gave me moments I'll always treasure. To every coach, every member of staff, to my team-mates and everyone who supported me, thank you for believing in me and helping me grow.

"As I begin my next chapter, Manchester United will always be a part of my story. Thank you for everything. JJ."

Gabriel did not make a competitive first-team appearance for United, but did make a senior appearance in United's pre-season friendly defeat to AC Milan this summer.

The teenager requested to leave United in September and has not trained with the club since. When Gabriel's camp made it clear he wanted to move on, they asked to be de-registered immediately for the player to become a free agent.

Sky Sports News understands a big factor as to why Gabriel is leaning towards a move to Real Madrid is because he and his camp are confident there is a clear pathway to the first team at the Bernebeu.

Follow Sky Sports on WhatsApp for the latest sports news, videos, features, analysis and much more

"Those on the outside of Carrington's walls will understandably view Gabriel's impending departure as a major blow for Manchester United. The reality, however, is that it has been expected for some time."Gabriel and his family have no longstanding ties to United as a club. He was not born and raised in the north of England, where a sense of local connection might have carried greater weight in his decision to stay."Over the last 12 months, United have pulled out all the stops to convince Gabriel and his family that there was a pathway to the first team. They met Sir Alex Ferguson, Ruben Amorim and Michael Carrick. Gabriel also trained with the senior squad and made his first-team debut at the age of 15 against Atletico Madrid in pre-season."Ultimately, it was not enough to keep him at the club. As disappointing as it will be to lose a talent of Gabriel's calibre, United will no longer have to make further concessions to accommodate a young player and his family - a situation that can risk upsetting the balance of an entire academy system."There is a wider consideration, too. Parents and agents connected to other academy players will inevitably take note when they see one individual receive such tailored treatment. It can create expectations that are difficult for a club to manage across its youth system."This situation now appears to be reaching its conclusion. But the focus will soon shift to the 16-year-old himself. His talent is undeniable, but will the circus surrounding his exit become a hindrance to his development, or will it prove to be little more than a footnote in what could be a long and illustrious career?"Time will tell. And I imagine Jose Mourinho will have something to say about it, too."

"This kid's a good player. He could have been a superstar. Man Utd will be gutted.

"Manchester United have worked on this kid, made him the player he is. Obviously, he'd have been gifted at the same time.

"The thing is, he's going to Real Madrid. I said Manchester United are one of the biggest clubs in the world. He's going to the biggest club in the world. It's not like there's not a player in the world who wouldn't go to Real Madrid.

"You ask any player who's playing in the Premier League, if Real Madrid come for you, it's very rare you turn them down. Great opportunity for the kid."

WoW Forever players say Twitch streamers are getting away with the same gold trick that got them banned

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

World of Warcraft: Forever players say Blizzard banned them for using a gold farm that Twitch streamers including Ziqo used, and they want to know why those streamers could keep playing.

On November 17, a player who goes by Lain on X said they woke up to find their account closed after farming Blackmaw Hold. The notice they shared says Blizzard permanently closed their Battle.net account and all associated game accounts for violating its end-user license agreement.

Lain attributed the closure to the farm and said they accepted the punishment, but questioned why Ziqo and other streamers were still able to play.

Hey @AggrendWoW @Warcraft I woke up to an account closure for farming blackmaw hold. Why the double standard? Do you intend to only ban people who aren't streamers? I accept my punishment, but it's ridiculous that ziqo and others can continue playing while my account is closed https://t.co/1bp3T4G15n pic.twitter.com/Oi2cxCMVjJ

The farm centered on a room in Blackmaw Hold where players could repeatedly clear a group of enemies and collect their loot. According to Wowhead, the method brought in more than 100 gold an hour before Blizzard removed those enemies’ loot drops.

In one clip, Ziqo said he was going to “abuse the sh*t outta this before it gets banned.” In another, he showed more than 4,000 gold in his inventory and said he could have reached 5,000 if he’d skipped four hours of sleep. How much of that gold came from Blackmaw Hold is unclear.

Ziqo confirmed on X that he had farmed Blackmaw Hold and disputed that it was an exploit. He linked to loot tables for the dungeon enemies and a weaker enemy found out in the world, arguing that they dropped the same items.

Declan Rice’s new five-year deal to 2031 marks his rise from £105m signing to Arsenal leader

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Alexis Pereira | 17 Nov 2026 18:28

Declan Rice’s new contract with Arsenal recognises a transformation built over three seasons. When he arrived at the Emirates Stadium in July 2023, the midfielder was a high-impact signing: he cost £105m, a record for a British player at the time, and carried the expectation of lifting a team that had just lost the Premier League title to Manchester City.

Today, at 27, Rice is an English champion, one of the leaders of the dressing room and one of the team’s technical reference points. The new five-year deal shows Arsenal’s intention to keep him at the centre of the project through his physical and technical peak. It also reflects his changing status within the club, as Rice is expected to earn a salary close to that of Bukayo Saka, until now the squad’s top earner.

Rice was not just an expensive signing who lived up to expectations. He became a more complete player, widened his influence across different phases of play and took on responsibilities beyond his role in midfield. The promise of 2023 has become one of the faces of a championship team and a leader for the cycle that starts now.

Rice’s arrival came at a decisive moment for Mikel Arteta’s project. In 2022-23, Arsenal spent much of the season at the top but lost steam in the closing stretch and finished five points behind Manchester City. The team had shown that they could compete for the title, but they needed more quality, depth and experience to sustain a challenge to the end.

Rice had characteristics that answered several of those needs. At 24, he was already West Ham United’s captain, had experience with England and had just won the Conference League. Arsenal beat the competition and completed the signing for £100m fixed plus £5m in bonuses.

The fee raised expectations. It was not enough for Rice to be a good holding midfielder: he had to justify a historic investment and help Arsenal take the step needed to compete with City. His first season showed the scale of the signing quickly.

Rice played 38 Premier League games, scoring seven goals and providing eight assists. The number of goal contributions was a considerable improvement on his West Ham record, but his impact was not limited to attacking output. Arsenal again finished as runners-up, but Rice had already become one of the team’s most important players. The signing did not deliver an immediate title, but it helped turn expectation into a conviction that the club had found a midfielder able to carry their project for many years.

Rice’s development also reflects the way Arteta changed his use. Early in his time at Arsenal, the Englishman often played as the deepest midfielder, responsible for protecting the defence and offering a reference point for build-up. It was a familiar role, close to the one he had at West Ham, but it did not use his full repertoire.

After an adaptation period, especially from the start of 2024, Arteta began using him more often as a box-to-box midfielder on the left. The change let Rice use one of his main qualities: carrying the ball at speed, breaking lines and arriving in the opposition area while still contributing to defensive recovery.

The Englishman gained more freedom to attack space, appear at the edge of the box and join attacking combinations. His physical power became a weapon for taking Arsenal into the opposition half, while his ability to get back helped keep balance when the team lost the ball. Instead of just protecting team-mates, Rice also began to drive the team.

Set pieces were another important tool. Rice began taking corners and free-kicks, using the accuracy of his delivery and his ability to put the ball in dangerous areas. Since his arrival, he has provided ten assists from set pieces in the Premier League. His two free-kick goals against Real Madrid in the 2024-25 Champions League quarter-finals were the most emblematic moment of that evolution.

Versatility does not mean Rice has no fixed position or needs to take part in every attacking action. The most recent development lies in choosing moments. With Martin Zubimendi, Myles Lewis-Skelly and Martin Odegaard offering different partnership options, Arteta has alternated the height at which the Englishman receives the ball and the freedom he has to advance.

The ability to perform different roles is one of the reasons Rice has become so important to Arteta. In the title-winning season, he was used both as the most advanced midfielder and in a midfield pair alongside Zubimendi. He is not just a player who adapts to different positions: he lets the manager change the structure of the team without necessarily changing personnel.

Rice’s growth was not only tactical. Over the seasons, he also became one of the squad’s main voices. His direct personality, willingness to demand more from team-mates and ability to take responsibility in difficult moments made him a reference point for the group.

One example came in April, when Odegaard was out injured and Rice was given the captain’s armband. He had worn it on other occasions, but the choice reinforced a perception that had been building: his influence does not depend on being the official captain.

In Arsenal’s 2025-26 Premier League title, which ended a 22-year wait for the competition, Rice was one of the protagonists of the campaign, with 36 appearances, four goals and five assists in the league. More than his statistical contribution, his ability to maintain intensity in different roles was fundamental.

Declan Rice at Arsenal - all competitions since joining in July 2023 2023-24: 51 games, 48 starts, 33 wins, 8 draws, 10 defeats, 7 goals, 9 assists (64.7% wins) 2024-25: 52 games, 48 starts, 28 wins, 16 draws, 8 defeats, 9 goals, 10 assists (53.8% wins) 2025-26: 55 games, 49 starts, 37 wins, 12 draws, 6 defeats, 5 goals, 9 assists (67.3% wins) 2026-27 (in progress): 15 games, 14 starts, 13 wins, 1 draw, 1 defeat, 1 goal, 5 assists (86.7% wins) Total: 173 games, 159 starts, 111 wins, 37 draws, 25 defeats, 22 goals, 33 assists (64.2% wins)

Trophies for Arsenal (3): Premier League 2025-26, Community Shield 2023, Community Shield 2026

Official matches only (Premier League, Champions League, FA Cup, EFL Cup and Community Shield). Matches decided on penalties count as draws. Data up to 16 November 2026. Sources: FBref (Opta data), BeSoccer and Opta Analyst.

The challenge now is to sustain this level without ignoring fatigue. Rice has come from seasons with a high volume of games, often exceeding 50 matches for club and country. Last season, he played 54 games for Arsenal in all competitions, in addition to England commitments. Managing his workload will be important for him to keep his influence over a demanding calendar.

By extending his contract to 2031, Arsenal secure a player who still has years at his peak ahead and who already knows Arteta’s demands in depth. The club no longer need to wait for Rice to become a leader: that stage has already happened. Now the expectation is that he helps lead the next chapters of a project that intends to keep competing for the main trophies.

MLB rumors: Mets meet with Tarik Skubal, Red Sox getting calls for multiple players, plus Cubs, Red Sox, more

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The annual GM Meetings came and went last week with two significant transactions. The Braves signed reliever Evan Phillips (three years and $35 million) and the Red Sox signed slugger Jake Bauers (four years and $58 million). Bauers will give Boston some desperately needed home run power. Phillips will pair with Robert Suarez in the late innings for Atlanta.

Otherwise, we're still waiting on the winter's first true blockbuster, and the clock is ticking with a lockout expected upon the collective bargaining agreement's expiration at 11:59 p.m. ET on Dec. 1. Here are our top 50 free agents and top 25 trade candidates, and the important offseason dates and deadlines you need to know. Here now are Tuesday's hot stove happenings.

Teams are calling the Rangers about left-hander MacKenzie Gore, who is a year away from free agency, according to The Athletic. There is some reluctance to trade for players with only one year of control because games could be lost to a work stoppage next year, but pitching is so in-demand that at least some clubs are exploring a deal for Gore.

Now 27, Gore is one of only six pitchers to make at least 30 starts in each of the last three years. His under-the-hood numbers (3.87 FIP and 4.19 expected ERA) were much better than his 4.58 actual ERA, plus he has long been considered to have untapped upside. Gore will give you innings, at minimum, and there's a chance his best days are ahead of him.

The Red Sox are exploring the trade market for righties Kutter Crawford, Tanner Houck, and Johan Oviedo, according to WEEI. They are also believed to be preparing to trade Jarren Duran, reports MassLive.com. Duran is a potential non-tender candidate if the Red Sox are unable to find a trade partner prior to Friday's deadline. The Mariners and Phillies are among the teams that are "poking around."

Crawford, Houck, and Ovideo all missed significant time with injuries in 2026 (Crawford has not appeared in an MLB game since 2024) and are far down Boston's rotation depth chart. Duran has been pushed out of the everyday lineup by the recent Jake Bauers signing. Exploring trades for these four players in a seller's market is a sensible approach.

The Mets had an in-person meeting with top-ranked free agent Tarik Skubal on Monday, reports the New York Post. Owner Steve Cohen and president of baseball operations David Stearns were among those in attendance, according to SNY. Skubal and agent Scott Boras will meet with the Blue Jays, Dodgers, Padres, and potentially two other teams in the coming days.

Skubal is expected to sign before the lockout and is therefore working on a tight schedule, much tighter than usual for top Boras clients. Nolan McLean and Christian Scott give the Mets two promising young arms to build around, but they need to improve the rotation around them, and Skubal would give the club a no-doubt ace to lead the pitching staff.

The pitching-needy Cubs have shown interest in free agent lefty Trevor Rogers, reports The Athletic. Chicago needs multiple starters this offseason and has cast a wide net in their search. Justin Steele (UCL) and Ben Brown (neck), ostensibly the Cubs' No. 1 and 2 starters, are both recovering from surgeries, and Brown is expected to miss the start of 2027.

We ranked Rogers as the third-best free agent available this offseason because he is very good as is, and because he'll make analytical models hum with the quality of his arsenal and strike-throwing. Nine pitchers made at least nine starts for the Cubs in 2026 and only two -- Javier Assad and Edward Cabrera -- currently remain under contract/team control.

The AL East rival Orioles and Yankees are among the teams with interest in free agent slugger Randy Arozarena, according to the Baltimore Banner and New York Post. The O's also have some interest in re-signing Luis Robert Jr. Other than Dylan Beavers and maybe Tyler O'Neill, Baltimore's outfield is wide open right now. They have DH at-bats available too.

It is likely the Yankees checked in on Arozarena out of due diligence more than serious interest. His righty bat would help their lefty-heavy lineup, though he would be a clunky roster fit as a corner outfielder who really needs to DH on a team that already has plenty of corner outfielders who really need to DH. If Arozarena's market is slow to develop, perhaps New York would pounce.

Teams around the league believe the Rockies are more open to trading All-Star catcher Hunter Goodman now than they were at the trade deadline, reports MLB Network. Colorado is said to have set a very high asking price for Goodman, who in 2026 became just the tenth primary catcher with a 40-homer season. He hit more homers on the road (25) than at Coors Field (16) too.

Ryan Jeffers and Carson Kelly represent viable free agent alternatives to Goodman as right-handed offense-first catchers, though Goodman is several years younger than both and will have a lower salary the next three years through arbitration. Teams will likely feel more confident in Goodman holding his production for another few years than Jeffers and Kelly.

Ryo Asai’s ‘In the Megachurch’ Sweeps 3 Charts on Inaugural Billboard Japan Book Charts Year-End Lists

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Yuu Nagira's Nanji, Hoshi no Gotoku came in at No. 2, followed by Mina Miyajima's Naruse wa Tenka wo Tori ni Iku at No. 3.

Billboard Japan announced the winning titles and authors of the first annual Billboard Japan Book Charts, its comprehensive book charts, at the year-end awards ceremony held Saturday (Nov. 14). The tracking period for the year ran from Oct. 27, 2025, to Oct. 25, 2026, with the exception of the Hot Shot Books chart, which ranks surging titles, tracked from Nov. 24, 2025, to Oct. 25, 2026. Related Wale on Triumphant Pair of 2027 Grammy Nominations for 'Everything Is a Lot': 'I Hope It Teaches a Lot of People About Resilience' Miley Cyrus Reacts to Shared Grammy Nod With Dolly Parton: 'A Beautiful Way to Honor Her Enormous Heart' Rosalía Reacts to Her 9 Grammy Nominations: See How She Celebrated Explore Billboard See latest videos, charts and news

The ceremony was one of the programs at BOOK MEETS NEXT 2026’s special event exploring the possibilities of an award showcasing the appeal of Japanese books to a wider global readership. BOOK MEETS NEXT is the autumn reading promotion month organized by the Japan Publishing Industry Foundation for Culture (JPIC).

Ryo Asai’s In The Megachurch was No. 1 for the year on the all-genre Japan Book Hot 100 list. By metric, it placed No. 1 in social media, No. 7 in subscriptions and No. 13 in brick-and-mortar sales. The novel earned its most points in April, the month it won the 2026 Hon’ya Taisho (Japan Booksellers’ Award). Its points also rose during the year-end and New Year holidays, Golden Week and Silver Week, when demand for reading is high.

“I’m very grateful this book chart exists, because it adds one more way for books to reach the world,” Asai said. “From that point of view, it’s probably not a great thing for the world at large that the Japan Booksellers’ Award winner and the No. 1 book on this chart are the same title. But for me personally, it means a lot, and I’m really, really happy.”

Yuu Nagira’s Nanji, Hoshi no Gotoku came in at No. 2, followed by Mina Miyajima’s Naruse wa Tenka wo Tori ni Iku at No. 3, Eiichiro Oda’s ONE PIECE Vol. 113 at No. 4 and Asako Yuzuki’s BUTTER at No. 5. In The Megachurch also topped the Hot Bungei Books literary fiction genre chart, and the Reiwa Books era chart that groups titles by the year their first edition was published, for a total of three No. 1s.

Among the other genre charts, ONE PIECE Vol. 113 led the Hot Manga chart for the year. Ryo@Ribe Daigaku Gakucho’s Honto no Jiyu wo Te ni Ireru Okane no Daigaku topped the Hot Economy Books business chart, and Fuma Kikuchi 1st Photobook “Latido” led the Hot Culture Books chart.

On the era charts, Yukito Ayatsuji’s Jukkakukan no Satsujin took No. 1 on the Showa Books chart, while Asako Yuzuki’s BUTTER topped the Heisei Books chart. Jujutsu Kaisen ≡ (Jujutsu Kaisen Modulo) Vol. 1 by Gege Akutami and Yuji Iwasaki was No. 1 for the year on the Hot Shot Books chart, which picks out books whose total points grew by a set percentage and lists only the titles drawing a sudden surge in attention.

The Billboard Japan Book Charts are Japan’s first comprehensive book charts, aggregating data across print (brick-and-mortar and e-commerce), e-books, libraries and social media. While new manga releases with strong print sales tended to reach the upper tier of the weekly Japan Book Hot 100, literary works stood out near the top of the year-end chart, supported by steady reader activity such as library lending and word of mouth on social media. The full year-end results are available on the Billboard Japan website.

Humanity braces for an alien invasion in 3 Body Problem S2 teaser

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

“Wake up! They haven’t come 24 trillion miles to negotiate. They are coming to exterminate us.”

You might be excused for forgetting about the existence of 3 Body Problem, Netflix’s heady sci-fi series adapted from the award-winning novel The Three-Body Problem by Liu Cixin, the first book in his Remembrance of Earth’s Past trilogy. After all, it debuted in March 2024. But it was an exceptionally well-done, thematically rich series, and we’re finally getting the second of a planned three seasons in January. Netflix just dropped the first teaser.

Created by David Benioff, D.B. Weiss (Game of Thrones), and Alexander Woo (True Blood), the first season followed two primary timelines. In the earlier one, a young Chinese astrophysicist, Ye Wenjie (Rosalind Chao), witnessed her father being beaten to death by Red Guards during the Cultural Revolution. Ye figured out a means of sending an interstellar message to possible extraterrestrial civilizations, receiving a response from a planet called Trisolaris.

The planet had three suns, which wreaked havoc on Trisolaris via unpredictable “chaotic periods”—hence the novel’s title, which refers to a classic problem in celestial mechanics. Despite being warned that the aliens intended to invade and conquer Earth, Ye responded to the message and invited them to do so, bitter and disillusioned by the state of the world. She eventually became an established professor (Zine Tseng played the older version).

In the present day, the consequences of Ye’s fateful decision began to play out, beginning with a series of mysterious suicides by scientists, many of whom had possessed a highly advanced VR gaming headset. Among the dead: a particle physicist named Vera Ye (daughter of Ye Wenjie). Five of her former students (known as the Oxford Five) joined forces to figure out what was going on, eventually finding themselves caught in a global fight to save Earth from the invading Trisolarians (or San-Ti). Another human cult faction was just as intent on enabling the San-Ti invasion, believing they would be spared as a reward, while Ye Wenjie still believed the entire human race deserved to be annihilated.

The second season will adapt the second novel in the series, The Dark Forest, with the third book, Death’s End, serving as source material for the third season. Per the official S2 synopsis: “As the alien invasion nears, humanity prepares to meet them—on Earth and beyond. While a space fleet embarks on a mission that spans centuries, a secret locked inside of one man’s mind might be humanity’s only hope for survival.”

We lost two members of the Oxford Five in S1: Jack Rooney (John Bradley), killed by cult member Tatiana (Marlo Kelly); and physics teacher Will Downing (Alex Sharp), who was dying of cancer and chose to euthanize himself and donate his cryogenically frozen brain to a bold effort to stave off the invasion. (While the probe carrying Will’s brain veered off-course in the season finale, the original plan was for the San-Ti to use the brain to reconstruct his body, so some version of Will could show up again.)

Kelly’s Tatiana is returning for S2, along with surviving Oxford Five members Jin Cheng (Jess Hong), Saul Durand (Jovan Adepo), and Auggie Salazar (Eiza Gonzalez), whose pioneering nanofibers were memorably used in S1 to slice through a ship in the Panama Canal, killing all the pro-alien cult members on board in gruesome fashion. Also returning are Liam Cunningham as Thomas Wade; Benedict Wong as MI6 agent Clarence “Da” Shi; Sea Shimooka as Sophon, an avatar in the show’s mysterious VR game; and Saamer Usmani as Jin’s Royal Navy officer boyfriend Prithviraj Varma.

New cast members include Claudia Doumit as Captain Van Rijn and Ellie de Lange as Ayla. In addition, Alfie Allen, David Yip, and Jordan Sunshine will appear in as-yet-undisclosed guest roles.

The second season of 3 Body Problem drops on Netflix on January 14, 2027.

After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Obama-appointed judge paves way to unseal Ilhan Omar search warrant in blow to Trump's DOJ

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

White House 'border czar' Tom Homan joins 'America Reports' to discuss federal probes into Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., the threat of migrant caravans and more.

A federal judge in Washington, D.C. who has often ruled against the Trump administration dealt a blow to the Justice Department Tuesday, paving the way to unseal the criminal search warrant and related documents against Rep. Ilhan Omar.

Judge James Boasberg, who was appointed to the bench by former President Barack Obama in 2011, asked the DOJ's attorneys to submit proposed redactions to the documents to the court by Friday, and said he plans to unseal some materials Monday. Any parts of the warrant or related materials that are not submitted with redactions on Friday will be released, he said.

While criminal warrants aren't typically unsealed until after charges are filed, 15 media organizations and watchdog group Judicial Watch sued to obtain the warrant. They have argued that releasing the contents of the warrant is in the public interest.

Rep. Ilhan Omar poses for a portrait in her office on Capitol Hill on Dec. 10, 2025. (Demetrius Freeman/The Washington Post via Getty Images)

'RADICAL LEFT LUNATIC' JUDGE TRUMP WANTED IMPEACHED TAKES UP ILHAN OMAR WARRANT FIGHT

The plaintiffs argued specifically that it should be made public which statutes federal investigators think Omar may have broken.

Boasberg signaled that he would not release the warrant application, which is a sworn statement submitted to a judge explaining facts and evidence that establish probable cause for issuing a warrant.

The DOJ did not immediately return Fox News Digital's request for comment.

Omar is facing a probe related to whether she or her father fraudulently filled out immigration paperwork in order for her to obtain citizenship. The Somali-born congresswoman said last week that she wasn't privy to what exactly the DOJ is investigating.

During the raid, which was conducted by Homeland Security Investigations (HIS), Omar's personal cellphone, computer and passport were seized. Her DNA has also been obtained, and her sister, Sahra Noor, was questioned by investigators the week of the raid.

FEDERAL AGENTS SEIZE PHONE OF REP ILHAN OMAR IN RAID TIED TO IMMIGRATION FRAUD PROBE

Homeland Security Investigations police search the home of Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., on Oct. 12, 2026, in Washington, D.C. According to reports, the search is part of an ongoing criminal investigation. (Andrew Leyden/Getty Images)

In a motion seeking to prevent the federal search warrant for the congresswoman’s home from being unsealed, the U.S. Attorney's Office for the District of Columbia referred to Omar as "Ilhan Nur Said Elmi, a/k/a Ilhan Abdullahi Omar…" and sources later told Fox News Digital that Ilhan Omar is not the congresswoman's "real name."

That news caused speculation that Omar married her brother, Ahmed Nur Said Elmi, to resurface. The pair married in 2009, and officially divorced in 2017.

ILHAN OMAR APPEARS CLUELESS ON BASIS FOR FEDERAL PROBES: ‘YOU PROBABLY HAVE MORE DETAILS ON THAT’

Omar has not been charged with a crime and has repeatedly and categorically denied any wrongdoing. She has characterized the investigation as politically motivated, saying President Donald Trump is targeting her.

But the DOJ under former President Barack Obama opened an investigation into Omar's marriage with Nur Said Elmi in 2016 — suggesting the nonpartisan nature of some of the inquiries into the Minnesota lawmaker.

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., and Rep. Eric Swalwell, D-Calif., speak during a news conference on Capitol Hill on Jan. 25, 2023, in Washington, D.C. (Jabin Botsford/The Washington Post via Getty Images)

OBAMA BACKS ILHAN OMAR AMID PROBE, ACCUSES TRUMP ADMIN OF 'APPALLING' TARGETING IN LENGTHY STATEMENT

The New York Times first reported that Obama's FBI and Department of Homeland Security (DHS) began the probe in the summer of 2016, and that officials "found some evidence that [the marriage] was fraudulent." The Times reported that the investigation concluded when officials decided the potential felony crime was past the statute of limitations.

That probe, an official told Fox News Digital, remained open until 2021, when it was closed under the Biden administration.

‘Both utopians and the doomers are going to be disappointed’: Fed advisor Marc Andreessen on AI going from boom to bust

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Marc Andreessen got the last word at the Federal Reserve’s big AI conference in San Francisco on Monday, and he used it to tell the central bank that nearly everyone in the room was going to be let down.

“My line has been both utopians and the doomers are going to be disappointed,” the venture capitalist said at the San Francisco Fed, “specifically disappointed by the scope and magnitude of the real-world impact, and specifically the economic impact.”

Andreessen co-leads the Productivity and Jobs Task Force, which Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh set up to tell the central bank what super intelligence—“SI,” in the White House’s preferred term—will do to growth, prices and jobs. On Monday, Andreessen previewed what he’ll contribute to the group’s report. Without using the name, he described one of the oldest problems in economics: William Baumol’s “cost disease,” the reason flat-screen TVs get cheaper every year while hospital bills, tuition and rent go the other way. His case is that AI won’t cure it, and could even make it worse.

The Fed hasn’t quite laid out that outcome.

The task force is advisory, and Andreessen has no vote on interest rates. He co-leads it with Stanford economist Charles Jones and Microsoft executive Asha Sharma, one of five outside panels the Fed named in June to rethink how the central bank works. Its charge, per the Fed, is to assess the economic impact of “new general-purpose technologies, including super intelligence, to inform the Federal Reserve’s policy judgments.” The panels report to the rate-setting committee with recommendations due by year-end, though Warsh said in October that “some, but not all” would turn their homework in on time. The Fed’s task force page lists no ethics or recusal terms for the panels. Andreessen’s firm, Andreessen Horowitz, raised more than $15 billion in new funds in January, as venture firms raced to keep up with the AI boom.

Andreessen is also Silicon Valley’s best-known accelerationist. His 2023 “Techno-Optimist Manifesto” declared, “We believe in accelerationism,” which he defined as “the conscious and deliberate propulsion of technological development.” It listed @BasedBeffJezos, the pseudonymous account behind the “effective accelerationism,” or e/acc, movement, first among its “patron saints,” and it named the enemy: “Our enemy is stagnation.” When OpenAI’s board fired Sam Altman a month later, Andreessen posted simply “E/acc!” This is the man who told the Fed that stagnation might win out over an AI-powered industrial revolution on Monday.

Warsh opened the day by laying out three “illustrative scenarios” Fed board staff have begun to evaluate. In one, AI lifts productivity, the gains are broadly shared and the economy stays close to full employment. In another, growth still runs above its long-term average, but automation pushes unemployment higher. In the third, AI “could disappoint after significant capital investment and stock market gains,” stocks fall, the economy slows and it eventually recovers “without the productivity gains.”

All three turn on whether AI arrives in force. Andreessen’s version is duller: most of the economy just doesn’t let it in.

He splits the economy in two. The “fast sectors,” like consumer electronics, media and commodity goods, take up new technology quickly, and “what those sectors all have in common is they have rapidly falling prices.” The “slow sectors”—health care, education, housing, and law and government—“are not legally allowed to have rapid change, like literally not.”

The machine “cannot get an MD, cannot prescribe medication,” he said. “The machine cannot get admitted to the bar.” The slow sectors, in his telling, pair restricted supply with subsidized demand: you can’t easily build new housing or open new hospitals, and when voters revolt over the cost, Washington steps in with student loans and housing subsidies. “So, of course, prices can only go up.”

Then he followed it to its end. As prices crash in the fast sectors, they shrink as a share of GDP, and the slow sectors take over. Health care, he said, “is already a fifth of US GDP and I don’t see why it doesn’t go to a fourth, a third and then half and then ultimately 80%.” (The government’s count is 18% in 2024, or $5.3 trillion, per CMS actuaries.)

“You might interpret this as good news because it means the AI revolution is not going to, you know, destroy all the jobs or kill everybody,” he said. “You could also view this as very negative, which is productivity growth just simply may not grow very much. Economic growth just simply may not grow very much. We may just continue to stagnate.”

Andreessen called himself “extremely optimistic,” saying that AI gives the country “a magic formula” and comparing it to being “handed this elixir to boost productivity growth.” But policy is a problem, he suggested. The decisions about what to do with AI, he said, “are going to matter actually way more than what the technology is capable of,” because without them “the technology will simply not get rolled out.”

That landed in a room where, three hours earlier, Warsh had brought up “secular stagnation,” the 1930s worry that economists returned to after the financial crisis, and asked whether superintelligence “dramatically” changes that picture.

Baumol, the late New York University and Princeton economist, worked this out in the 1960s, starting with the economics of the performing arts. A string quartet needs the same four musicians and the same playing time it did in Mozart’s day, but those musicians have to be paid wages set by an economy where factory workers keep getting more productive. So live music, and later education and medicine, keep getting more expensive relative to everything else, and keep taking a bigger share of spending.

In his 2012 book, The Cost Disease, Baumol projected that if health costs kept growing at their recent pace, they would rise from 15% of the average person’s income in 2005 to 62% by 2105. He was an optimist about it. “No matter how painful rising medical and educational bills may be, society can afford them,” he wrote, because productivity elsewhere makes everyone richer. The real threat, he added, was “foolish public policy.”

For Baumol, the disease was built into the work: you can’t speed up a string quartet. For Andreessen, it’s built into the law. That matters for the Fed, because an agency that sets interest rates can’t fix zoning boards or medical licensing.

The economist who has done the most to bring Baumol into the AI debate was in the audience. Jones, who is on leave at Anthropic, stayed quiet because the company is preparing to go public, as Andreessen remarked to the crowd. In a 2017 paper for the National Bureau of Economic Research, Jones, Philippe Aghion and Benjamin Jones argued that “growth may be constrained not by what we are good at but rather by what is essential and yet hard to improve,” crediting the idea to Baumol.

San Francisco Fed President Mary Daly asked a panel of executives where their “weak link” was, and Erik Wexler, CEO of the nonprofit hospital system Providence, gave the slow-sector answer. “We’re part of the weak link,” he said. Providence operates in seven states, and as each writes its own AI rules, “it’s almost like sometimes being in seven different countries.”

Earlier on the same panel, though, Wexler complicated Andreessen’s picture. AI tools that listen in on appointments and write up the notes are saving some physicians “two to four hours of time a day,” he said, and doctors have called it “life-changing.” In 35 years in the industry, he said, he’d never heard them say that about anything else. So the hours are being saved inside a slow sector. The open question is whether they ever show up in the price of a hospital visit.

Andreessen’s forecast cuts against the case Warsh made before he got the job. In a Wall Street Journal op-ed late last year, Warsh wrote that AI “will be a significant disinflationary force, increasing productivity and bolstering American competitiveness,” as Axios reported. In speeches and writing, he argued AI-driven productivity could justify lower interest rates, an echo of Alan Greenspan’s Fed holding off on hikes in the late 1990s.

As chair, he has hedged. “My judgment is we have seen an improvement in productivity; we can’t measure it precisely,” he told reporters on Oct. 28, adding that “most of that change in productivity precedes everything we’re talking about—super intelligence.” The Fed held rates at 3.75% to 4% that day, and a reporter told him most of his colleagues “seem to be on board with raising rates in December.” Warsh declined to prejudge the meeting.

On Monday, Warsh didn’t push back on Andreessen. He restated the argument in Fed terms, as product prices versus services prices, and moved on. He closed the day “cautiously optimistic,” calling it “a hinge point for the US economy,” and said, “I’d rather have our problems than anyone else’s in the world.”

Andreessen has been making this case for years. In 2017 he warned that the slow sectors were “basically just going to eat the economy,” and in March 2023 he wrote that “AI is simply already illegal across most of the economy.” Back then the argument was a rebuttal to the job-loss doomers. On Monday, in front of the Fed chair, it came with the other half of the bargain: if the jobs are safe, the productivity boom may be stuck too.

For companies, the slow sectors are a line item. Workers now pay an average of $7,471 a year toward employer family health coverage, up 9% from 2025, according to KFF’s annual survey. That’s the part of the economy that, by Andreessen’s account, AI isn’t legally allowed to touch.

For the Fed, the question is what kind of disinflation AI brings, if any. Earlier in the day, Warsh asked a panel of economists whether AI might do to services what the China shock did to goods—“productizing services”—and push prices down. Stanford’s Nick Bloom said falling token costs made him “kind of bullish.” Stanford President Jonathan Levin gave Baumol’s answer without the name: health care and education are “the parts of the economy where the disinflationary pressures from automation have not hit,” where “the costs have continued to rise with wages.” Whether that changes depends on what people want, he said, “and there’s also regulation.”

If Andreessen is right, AI’s price cuts land mostly where prices were already falling, and services, where inflation has been hardest to bring down, stay out of reach.

Stripe CEO Patrick Collison sketched the range for the room. At one end sits the old complaint that new technology shows up “everywhere but in the productivity statistics,” a line that goes back to economist Robert Solow in 1987. At the other is a bet between two people Collison described as having “considerable influence” over AI, on whether global energy use doubles in a single month sometime in the next decade. Andreessen is betting on a third outcome: a decade where nothing that dramatic happens, and the prices Americans complain about most keep climbing.

Warsh has asked his task forces for recommendations by year-end, and the Fed’s next rate decision comes Dec. 9. Andreessen put the choice to the central bank as “a political economy” question: “what kind of pace of change do we want, how much productivity growth do we actually want?”

Sundance Institute Partners With Dior to Support Female Filmmakers Through New Initiatives

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ahead of the 2027 festival, the partners announced the creation of a new fellowship for mid-career storytellers, an award for bold new voices, a brunch to celebrate all of the above and an appearance by longtime Dior muse Natalie Portman.

In January, the Sundance Film Festival will present its first edition in a new home of Boulder, Colorado, and two months ahead of the anticipated launch, the team has revealed the first VIP guest being welcomed into the fold: Dior.

Parfums Christian Dior has officially partnered with Sundance Institute for the purpose of supporting female filmmakers and storytellers. In doing so, they’ve created the Miss Dior x Women at Sundance Institute Award as a way to recognize “a breakthrough cultural force whose bold vision is reshaping the filmmaking landscape.” The honor will be doled out to a talent on the rise as selected by a yet-to-be-announced curated committee and presented by longtime Dior ambassador Natalie Portman during January’s main event. Related Stories General News Steven Spielberg and Kate Capshaw's Hearthland Foundation Donates $3M to Sundance Institute (Exclusive) Movies Sundance Institute Taps Tracy Brady for Chief Marketing, Brand and Communications Role

Also under the pact, they’ve created the Women at Sundance Institute Miss Dior Momentum Fellowship. The fellowship, created for “mid-career storytellers who demonstrate artistic excellence and a strong commitment to independent film, will support six artists on their second or third feature film projects through year-round support with direct non-recoupable grants, mentorship, coaching, cohort gatherings, bespoke creative support, networking and a curated Sundance experience. They’ll also get the true Sundance experience beyond Boulder through access to Sundance Institute educational and peer communities via the Artist Accelerator Program, ELEVATE professional development sessions, Sundance Collab opportunities and the Sundance Institute alumni network.

But wait, there’s more: the Sundance and Dior initiative launches will be the toast of a new event set to take place during the 2027 film festival — the Women’s Celebration Brunch presented by Dior. That’s where Portman will present the prize in front of a gathering of film industry insiders, press, creators and other VIP guests.

“The stories we tell have the power to shape how we see ourselves and one another. This is why supporting women storytellers is so important, not only because their voices deserve to be heard, but because they bring perspectives that enrich our understanding of the world. When a belief in emotion, independence, and conviction meets a commitment to authentic storytelling, we create more than opportunities for artists; we help ensure that women continue to shape culture through the stories they choose to tell,” said Portman, who serves as the face of the Miss Dior fragrance. “For me, Miss Dior has always represented the courage to embrace your own voice with optimism, independence, and conviction. I’m proud to see this spirit come to life through the partnership with Sundance Institute, celebrating women whose creativity will continue to inspire future generations.”

Added Michelle Satter, founding senior director of artist programs for the Sundance Institute: “At Sundance Institute, we believe every woman deserves the freedom to tell her story in her own voice, on her own terms. This shared commitment comes to life through the inaugural Miss Dior x Women at Sundance Institute Award, honoring a filmmaker whose creative vision makes a meaningful impact, and our ongoing Momentum Fellowship, which supports women directing their second or third fiction or nonfiction feature. Together, we’re investing in the voices, visions and stories that will move our culture forward. We look forward to celebrating this partnership at the Women’s Celebration Brunch presented by Dior during our upcoming Sundance Film Festival in Boulder.”

Film Adaptation of ‘Saint Philomene’s Infirmary for Magical Creatures’ Set from Danny Daneau, Laura C. Lopez

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Daneau and Lopez will pen the screenplay for the family film project from Inevitable Studios and Give Not Take Media.

Saint Philomene’s Infirmary for Magical Creatures, the middle-grade fantasy novel from Bill Cotter, is getting the feature film treatment from writers Danny Daneau and Laura C. Lopez and producers Inevitable Studios and Give Not Take Media.

Rehab Entertainment, Black Bar Mitzvah and independent producer Sydney Foos are also behind the project, which is being planned as a four-quadrant family feature.

The story follows a twelve-year-old boy struggling with a new school and a chronic illness who finds a passageway to an enormous subterranean hospital built to care for ailing monsters and magical creatures. He is called upon to help combat a dark force that threatens the peaceful institution. Related Stories Movies Sundance Institute Partners With Dior to Support Female Filmmakers Through New Initiatives Movies 'Hershey' Review: Finn Wittrock Plays the American Chocolate Titan in a Sickly Sweet Biopic

Inevitable Studios is the production banner housed within the entertainment and disability-focused Inevitable Foundation. Co-founded by Richie Siegel and Marisa Torelli-Pedevska, the goal of Inevitable Studios is to back film and television that is told through a disability lens.

Give Not Take Media is a multimedia entertainment company owned by Caring Across Generations, a national organization focused on caregivers and care recipients, that develops entertainment projects that engage with the experiences of caregiving. Give Not Take Media was behind the recent festival standout Take Me Home, which screened at both Sundance and Tribeca.

Terissa Kelton and John W. Hyde‘s Rehab Entertainment recently produced Apple‘s Harriet the Spy and its current slate includes a live-action Disney relaunch of Flight of the Navigator.

Black Bar Mitzvah, headed by Jay Ellis and Aaron Bergman, is the production company behind Amazon’s Black Box.

Lopez and Daneau are repped by M88 and HJTH.

New 'Pokémon' TCG Lucario Set Leaks In Stores Months Before Official Release

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Dollar General customers claim they've purchased the unreleased Pokémon set from shelves

The Pokémon Trading Card Game's latest set, Delta Reign, just came out a couple of weeks ago in early November. The next set, Flowing Fists, isn't scheduled to drop for another few months. Most people haven't seen Delta Reign boxes in stores yet, never mind the recent Pokémon 30th Anniversary set. Despite all of this, real people have apparently already acquired packs of Flowing Fist. Heck, some people have already pulled the next set's chase card — which hasn't even been formally revealed yet!

Pokémon leaks happen all the time, but in many instances, they seem to be the result of a potential theft or some kind of warehouse shenanigans. Maybe a factory worker sneaked a picture, or the set was close enough that distribution had already begun behind the scenes. This case may not be the same, judging from reports on social media.

Pokémon fans have shared evidence of going into stores like Dollar General and buying several blister packs with a Tynamo promo card straight off the shelves. One alleged DG customer produced a receipt with the Flowing Fists purchase. While a receipt could ostensibly be faked, multiple other people have apparently also purchased packs at Dollar General locations.

At least one early set recipient attempted to sell a special rare illustration card from Flowing Fists featuring Mega Lucario Z-EX, a Pokémon that debuted in the Mega Dimension DLC for Pokémon Legends: Z-A. The Flowing Fists set is the fighting type's first appearance in the card game.

The listing has since been pulled from eBay, but it does give us a good look at what the next set has to offer. The card, which is illustrated by prolific TCG artist Rend, depicts Lucario in the middle of a jump. The collectible appears to have a new type of engraving detail on the left side of Lucario that emphasizes his Aura Seeker attack. The set as a whole is themed around the fan-favorite monster, and is known to include new cards for Riolu as well as double-rare EX cards for Lucario.

In one video where a TCG fan opens a Flowing Fists pack early, he pulls a Kubfu by Hyogonosuke, a Hattrem, a Wartortle, and a Weepinbell by Ikeda Saki.

Notably, none of these cards have been officially revealed yet by The Pokémon Company. So far, we've only seen reveals for monsters like Oricorio, Pawmi and Mega Absol Z-EX among others. While most cards from the Japanese version of the set are put in their Western counterparts, The Pokémon Company sometimes alters the set list regionally.

The early sales snafu does not appear to be widespread. Many speculate it may have been a distribution error — and one that will be fixed quickly. So don't expect to find a Flowing Fists pack in the wild, especially now that the Meowth is out of the bag!

"My first layoff in 14 years making games"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Yet another round of layoffs has apparently gone down at Tomb Raider studio Crystal Dynamics, as developers report that they've been cut from the studio. This is at least the sixth round of layoffs since the studio last shipped an original game.

Multiple developers have posted on LinkedIn about the layoffs, as reported by Vertical Slice. Those include UI software engineer Garin R. K. Richards and senior game designer Joshua Mitchell. "After several rounds of layoffs at Crystal Dynamics, today it was my turn," Mitchell writes. "My first layoff in 14 years making games."

Also cut is senior environment technical artist Kevin Baker, who led the development of "procedural tools used extensively in the creation of Tomb Raider: Catalyst's game world," and "integrated Claude Code into procedural tool development." We already knew that Legacy of Atlantis is being made with AI, and it seems the studio is keen to use similar generative tech in Catalyst – though not keen enough to keep the people responsible for it employed, I guess.

Crystal Dynamics has not shipped a new game since the release of Marvel's Avengers in 2020. Since then, it was acquired by Embracer Group, a parent company that's undergone no shortage of financial upheaval in recent years. In 2023, Embracer insisted that Crystal Dynamics would be unaffected by the ongoing layoffs at the broader company, a few months before laying off people at the Tomb Raider studio.

Counting today's cuts, there have been five more rounds of layoffs since then. There were three sets of cuts in 2025 alone, both before and after Perfect Dark – which Crystal Dynamics had been co-developing – was cancelled at Xbox. The studio insisted it was still "fully committed" to its upcoming Tomb Raider games when it announced yet more layoffs in March 2026.

The extent of today's cuts are not yet fully clear, nor is their effect on the new games. All three developers who've posted about the layoffs so far listed Catalyst among their credits.

Jensen Huang says he would be 'honoured' to return as Nvidia CEO if board asked him

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang said he would be "honoured" to return as the boss of tech giant Nvidia if the board asked him to, in his first public comments since his shock resignation.

"I created problems for the Board that I deeply regret. I own my mistakes completely," Huang said on X, days after reports he had extramarital affairs, including with an alleged Chinese spy.

He said he had an "outpouring" of calls from company employees, investors and others calling for him to return as CEO of Nvidia.

But, as the BBC reported on Monday, Nvidia's board is not considering bringing him back to lead the company.

The prospect of Huang returning at this point looks unlikely.

The company's board is investigating his conduct, which includes an alleged affair with an Nvidia executive.

The AI chip giant is set to report its financial results later on Tuesday, during which the company's interim chief executive, Collette Kress, may address the situation with Huang.

Beyond the internal Nvidia investigation, Huang's affair with a woman suspected of being an asset for Beijing is set to see the billionaire questioned before multiple committees of US lawmakers in the weeks ahead.

The Nvidia founder said he had "made serious mistakes in my personal life".

Nonetheless, Huang's statement appeared to show some hope he would be able to move past the major corporate scandal.

Huang said people have continued to "reach out to me directly as the company navigates complex technology, market, competitive, and geopolitical dynamics".

"If the board of directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honoured to answer these calls and return as CEO," Huang added.

Many technology executives in recent days have praised Huang's role in the artificial intelligence boom. A petition began circulating online over the weekend calling for his reinstatement at the company.

Yet, by Monday, many executives were not openly calling for Huang's return.

Brad Gerstner, a major tech investor who knows Huang personally and also holds a significant stake in Nvidia, last week wrote an open letter saying that Nvidia could consider Huang's reinstatement.

On Monday, Gerstner said during an event hosted by Semafor that his letter had been misunderstood and the investigation into Huang's conduct needed to run its course.

While Gerstner questioned the facts around Huang's departure, he added: "I don't know to what degree he misled the board. If I was on a board I would take that very seriously. Let's be clear no one is above the law."

Jensen Huang Says He'd Be 'Honored' to Return as Nvidia CEO, Despite Board's Resistance

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang Says He'd Be 'Honored' to Return as Nvidia CEO, Despite Board's Resistance

Jensen Huang said Tuesday he would be willing to return as Nvidia CEO, publicly addressing the campaign to reinstate him less than a week after his abrupt departure. His offer came despite reports that the chipmaker's board has no plans to bring him back.

“If the Board of Directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honored to answer these calls and return as CEO,” Huang wrote in a statement on X.

The Nvidia co-founder also apologized for the conduct that triggered his exit. “I did not live up to the standards expected of everyone at NVIDIA, and I have made serious mistakes in my personal life,” he wrote. “I created problems for the Board that I deeply regret.”

“I own my mistakes completely, and I am committed to cooperating with the Board’s independent investigation,” he added.

Over the past week, I’ve been overwhelmed by the love and support of friends, industry colleagues, and people I've never met. Thank you to everyone who has shown kindness to my family and me. It means the world to me.

I came to the United States 53 years ago, and becoming an…

— Jensen Huang (@JensenHuang) November 17, 2026

His X profile underwent a separate change around the same time. By Tuesday, Huang had removed “CEO” from his bio, which now identifies him only as “Founder of NVIDIA”; the company banner and corporate affiliation badge also disappeared.

Huang resigned last Thursday after 33 years leading the chipmaker he founded in 1993. His departure followed disclosures about extramarital relationships involving Nvidia employees and a business executive whom U.S. officials suspect of connections to Chinese intelligence. Those intelligence allegations have not been established as fact.

The board's independent investigation is examining his conduct and whether sensitive company information or national security were put at risk. Nvidia has said it has no reason to believe company secrets were compromised; chief financial officer Colette Kress is serving as interim CEO.

“Since my departure, I’ve seen an outpouring of calls from NVIDIA’s employees, investors, business partners, and customers for me to return as CEO,” Huang wrote. He said many were reaching out directly as Nvidia navigates “complex technology, market, competitive, and geopolitical dynamics.” “NVIDIA is a once-in-a-generation company,” he added.

But the board's position—at least as of Tuesday—is that directors are focused on the investigation, not reinstatement.

The company is simultaneously looking for a successor. Directors reportedly approached Arm CEO Rene Haas, a former Nvidia executive, according to the Financial Times, although Nvidia said it had not formally engaged candidates and called speculation premature.

The movement to restore Huang has nevertheless attracted powerful allies. President Donald Trump said Monday he “would certainly not mind” a comeback, while Elon Musk backed a petition demanding Huang's reinstatement that had drawn nearly 192,000 signatures by Monday.

OpenAI CEO Sam Altman offered a more qualified defense. Altman, who was briefly fired and reinstated at OpenAI in 2023, said Huang had “long been a hero of mine” and that “heroes have flaws,” but stopped short of saying Nvidia should rehire him.

Huang also made a patriotic appeal, especially considering his outsting was especially rushed not because of his affairs but because of the possibility of him leaking secrets to China. “I came to the United States 53 years ago, and becoming an American citizen is one of the greatest gifts and proudest moments of my life,” he wrote.

“NVIDIA has been my life’s work,” he continued. “Nowhere but in America could a company like NVIDIA be possible. I love our country, and I love NVIDIA.”

He closed by thanking supporters and telling his family, “I love you, and I will spend the rest of my life being worthy of yours.” Nvidia, meanwhile, is continuing its permanent CEO search, with Kress scheduled to lead the company's quarterly earnings call later today.

Satya Nadella kicks off Microsoft Ignite with an Outlook outage mea culpa: ‘We have work to do’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Todd Bishop on Nov 17, 2026 at 10:23 amNovember 17, 2026 at 10:23 am

Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella opened his Ignite keynote Tuesday in San Francisco by addressing the elephant in the room: the global Outlook outage that cut off email for people and organizations in more than 70 countries last week.

Before getting to Copilot, agents or any of the event’s news, Nadella said the outage was a reminder of how critical digital infrastructure has become as “the dial tone for the world.”

“We know the trust you place in our services and take that responsibility extremely seriously,” he said. “And in that context, I know we have work to do to provide that resilience that you can count on, the world can count on, and we are committed to ensuring that we learn and we continuously improve.”

The line drew applause from the crowd, interrupting Nadella as he moved to thank the IT pros in the audience who kept their own organizations running through the outage.

It was a brief acknowledgment, and he didn’t go into what went wrong. Microsoft’s preliminary report blamed a bug in a fix it began rolling out in July, which its own automatic rerouting spread until most of the servers in a key Microsoft 365 gateway stopped responding. A final report is due Nov. 20.

From there, Nadella moved on to the main show, calling Copilot “the new operating system for work.” The keynote is ongoing. Watch it above, and stay tuned for updates.

BMW’s first US-built EV promises 456 miles of range and ultra-fast charging

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The iX5 is BMW’s first fully electric vehicle built in the US, promising an estimated up to 456 miles of range, ultra-fast charging speeds, and plenty of Neue Klasse tech.

BMW officially opened its new Plant Woodruff in South Carolina on November 13, marking the start of series production of its sixth-generation (Gen6) electric vehicle batteries.

The Gen6 batteries will power BMW’s upcoming “X” electric vehicles, starting with the all-new iX5 SUV.

The iX5 is being built about 15 miles away at BMW Group Plant Spartanburg, the company’s first fully electric model to be assembled in the US.

BMW Plant Spartanburg is churning out around 70 iX5s a day following a $1.7 billion investment in South Carolina to expand the plant for EV production and build the new battery assembly plant in Woodruff.

According to Robert Engelhorn, president and CEO of BMW Manufacturing in Spartanburg, the company is confident the iX5 will attract buyers, promising 435 miles of range, ultra-fast charging, and its updated Neue Klasse technology and design.

Engelhorn told Automotive News in an interview that “there’s a clear trend globally for EVs,” adding that “We will contribute to this growth from the BMW side.”

BMW didn’t provide a specific production estimate for the iX5, but suppliers were reportedly told it would be around 60,000 EVs in 2027, down from 100,000 it initially forecasted.

“We said we would begin assembling fully electric BMW vehicles in the US by the end of 2026 – and we are delivering on that commitment,” Engelhorn said.

A company spokesperson confirmed that the iX5 will initially use battery cells from China before switching to US-sourced cells from LG Energy Solutions plant in Arizona by the end of the decade.

With production now underway, the iX5 is expected to begin arriving at US dealerships in early 2027.

The 2027 BMW iX5 60 xDrive starts at $79,800. BMW estimates the 144 kWh battery will provide up to 456 miles of range, up from the 435 miles it previously estimated.

It features BMW’s new “double-X” light signature, which combines the headlights, daytime running lights, side lights, and turn signals into one unit. The Double-X headlights come standard, but BMW said you can switch them for simpler diagonal stripes.

Last month, BMW showcased the i5’s ultra-fast-charging capabilities during a media event in Southern California with IONNA and Alpitronic.

During the demo, the iX5 60 xDrive hit a peak of 464 kW, which BMW said enables it to add an impressive up to 170 miles of range in 10 minutes.

The electric SUV features a dual-motor all-wheel-drive powertrain setup that delivers a combined 578 hp (425 kW) and 593 lb-ft of torque. BMW says it can go from 0 to 60 mph in 4.4 seconds and tow up to 6,000 lbs.

Inside, the iX5 adopts BMW’s Neue Klasse design and tech, like the smaller iX3 SUV. The setup includes a new 17.9″ infotainment touchscreen and BMW’s Panoramic Vision, which spans the bottom of the windshield. There’s also an optional 14.6″ passenger screen for a third display.

Measuring 197.2″ long, 78.7″ wide, and 68.9″ tall, BMW’s new iX5 is about the size of the Porsche Cayenne Electric.

By 2030, BMW plans to be assembling at least fully electric models at Plant Spartanburg. “This marks the beginning of the next chapter in the success story of the largest plant in our global production network,” Raymond Wittman, BMW’s head of production, said.

Following Debrecen (Hungary), Irlbach-Straßkirchen (Germany), and Shenyang (China), Woodruff is the fourth site in BMW’s production network to begin series production of the Gen6 batteries.

GTA 6 Leads TGA Nominations, And Reviews Aren’t Even Live Yet

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The Keighleys, or more formally, The Game Awards, are right around the corner. Ahead of its December 10 air date, host Geoff Keighley announced the nominees for the myriad categories, including Game of the Year. Rockstar Games' Grand Theft Auto 6 made the cut, but in an unusual circumstance, it did so before any reviews of the game have been published.

Before anyone has read any official take on GTA 6, Rockstar Games' action-adventure game has eight nominations, making it the most-nominated game at this year's The Game Awards.

On top of being spotlighted for Game of the Year, the game also has nominations for Best Art Direction, Best Audio Design, Best Game Direction, Best Action/Adventure, Best Narrative, Best Performance (for Manni L. Perez's portrayal of Lucia), and Best Score, bringing the crime sim's total nominations to eight.

We already knew GTA 6 would be eligible for this year's ceremony. After all, per the TGA FAQ, the cutoff date for eligibility is November 20, one day after the game comes out. With a "jury of over 100 global media and influencer outlets" selecting the nominees, and many of these folks likely having plenty of hands-on time with Rockstar Games' latest release, it isn't much of a shocker to see GTA 6 at the top spot. Still, it is a little awkward that it has so many nominations before its release that may reveal what to expect from the critical reception..

Maybe it shouldn't be too surprising or weird, though. After all, the game isn't officially out yet, and it's already sold approximately 16 million copies and generated nearly $2 billion in revenue. Maybe that expensive sick day caused by the GTA Flu won't be so expensive.

Pokémon Go’s Most Cursed Crossover Officially Revealed

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Ready to learn a new language with Pikachu?

Pokémon Go has had its share of crossovers with other brands over the past decade, including The North Face x Gucci, Major League Baseball, and Lego. The mobile gotta-catch-'em-all game's latest will bring a new critter to the game, one known to strike fear in the hearts of mobile users.

As the Fortnite-driven crossover era continues, Pikachu is making his way to the language-learning app Duolingo. The first trailer for the app's crossover with Pokémon Go shows Duo, the app’s owl mascot, finally deciphering what Pikachu is saying.

The crossover between Duolingo and Pokémon Go starts on Nov. 17. Those who join the limited-time Duolingo event can earn up to four Pokémon-themed outfits for their Duolingo profile. The outfits include a Pikachu onesie, a Chatot suit, a Poké Ball mascot getup, and a Pokémon trainer wardrobe.

Unlike Duolingo’s recent unlockable outfit events, which rely on some random chance, Duo’s Pokémon Party event lets players earn guaranteed costumes by simply logging in and completing a daily lesson. Users have until Dec. 29, 2026, at midnight local time to complete a lesson on nine different days, with the final reward being the Pikachu suit.

Beyond the costumes and other in-game rewards, like gems, Duolingo users can also earn a Timed Research offer code for Pokémon Go after completing seven lessons on separate days. The code lets Pokémon Go players participate in an event where they can “put their new language skills to the test.”

“In this Timed Research, Trainers can practice a new language alongside Professor Willow while completing tasks in Pokémon Go,” a press release reads. “Choose a language, and the research tasks will appear in that language. Plus, keep an eye out for Professor Willow’s hints if you get stuck on a task!” Completing these tasks earns XP, Stardust, and encounters with event-themed Pokémon — Pikachu, Meowth, Chatot, and Lucario — with a special background.

Additionally, the update will bring a new feature called Language Tags, which appear on the summary page of Pokémon from the Timed Research event, showing their names in different languages. While this Duolingo collab seems quite random, Pokémon Go has been running all sorts of crossovers lately, from more predictable ones, like a tie-in with the Pokémon anime, to unexpected team-ups, like with Major League Baseball.

Official: Kean undergoing new medical assessment after Como setback

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Como have released a statement confirming Italy striker Moise Kean is ‘undergoing further medical assessment’ after an incident in training, as his return to action is further postponed.

The centre-forward has been struggling with a recurring tibia issue for many months, going back to his time at Fiorentina before the summer transfer to Como.

He played through pain, returned and had relapses, the most recent appearance coming in early October for Italy in the Nations League.

Kean had been given a recovery time of five weeks after specialist treatment, but there were reports of another setback in training.

“Following an issue during training, Moise Kean is undergoing further medical assessment,” confirmed a club statement this evening.

“Our priority is supporting his recovery. We will provide an update when we have further information to share.”

The tone of the statement from Como does suggest this could be another serious setback for the Italy international.

There are fears he could require a surgical intervention to resolve the issue once and for all, as conservative treatment has so far proved unsuccessful.

The 26-year-old has made six competitive appearances this season at club level for Fiorentina and Como, scoring one goal with one assist.

"Original Meaning and the Freedom of Speech and Press: Some Illustrative Examples"

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Mostly law professors | Sometimes contrarian | Often libertarian | Always independent

This short new article of mine has now been published in the Notre Dame Law Review. The Introduction:

There are many important big-picture questions about original meaning and the Free Speech, Free Press, Assembly, and Petition Clauses. This short Essay will leave those questions for others. Instead, it will offer as illustrations some specific, concrete questions—and, in some instances, answers—about original meaning and the freedom of expression. I hope these narrow illustrations can help us think about the broader questions.

From the table of contents, which should illustrate the subjects I cover:

I. Broad Evidence, and Silently Consistent Precedents A. "The Liberty of the Press Is Not Confined to Newspapers and Periodicals" B. Symbolic Expression C. Civil Liability II. Scant Evidence: Provoking a Riot III. Ambiguous Evidence: Enjoining Repetition of Libels

Whether courts should shift the constitutional law of free expression to a more originalist footing is an important and difficult question. Perhaps it's a question that should be resolved entirely as a matter of constitutional theory—maybe such an approach is required by proper interpretive principles, or maybe it's forbidden by other principles (for instance, having to do with precedent).

But it's also possible that the answer may rest in part on how practically usable an originalist framework is likely to be. And to consider that, it's helpful to have in mind some practical questions. Some are already familiar, for instance questions related to damages liability in libel cases, obscenity law, and the like. This short Essay has aimed to help supplement those familiar examples with some others that haven't been much discussed; I hope others who think about the topic can find them useful.

I expect I'll post excerpts from the article in the coming days.

NEXT: Court Ordered Social Media Personality to Stop Posting About Teenagers He Accused as to Nolan Wells' Death

Eugene Volokh is the Thomas M. Siebel Senior Fellow at the Hoover Institution at Stanford, and the Gary T. Schwartz Distinguished Professor of Law Emeritus and Distinguished Research Professor at UCLA School of Law. Naturally, his posts here (like the opinions of the other bloggers) are his own, and not endorsed by any institution. He is also the co-host of the Free Speech Unmuted podcast.
